H3 (lost-wakeup search) + H5 (thread accounting) hunter: optimus.graph.Hunter, probes/run-hunter.sh.
JDK 21, entity agent, loom-off build. Each (seed, configuration) is a child JVM; per-child logs under the scratchpad hunter/sweep3/.

Commands
  sweep (50 seeds x matrix, rerun every failure 5x, minimise N):  HUNTER_PAR=5 probes/run-hunter.sh sweep 1 50
  one seed/config:                                                 probes/run-hunter.sh one <seed> <cfg> [maxN] [reps]     e.g. probes/run-hunter.sh one 14 i2-a0-d0-lt1 0 5
  DAG summary for a seed:                                          probes/run-hunter.sh spec <seed>...
  deterministic reproducers:                                       probes/run-hunter.sh inv i1-a0-d0-lt1       (N3)
                                                                   probes/run-hunter.sh inv n2:i1-a0-d0-lt1    (N2)
  cfg = i{optimus.gthread.ideal}-a{optimus.scheduler.asserts 0|1}-d{delay 0|1}-lt{optimus.graph.limitThreads 0|1}
  d1 = -Dprobes.delay.maxNanos=50000: random 0..50 us parks at probes.DelayAgent's WorkQueue points AND at HunterAgent's
       scheduler/waiter points (Context.parked/awaitedTask/_spinningThreadID writes, before unpark, NodeTask._waiters reads
       and notify, runAndWait register/wait, run's tryRun); the point before LockSupport.park is a busy spin (see N1).
  knobs: -Dhunter.asyncMode=mixed|ena|eatc (how ASYNC launches its exclusive kids), hunter.budgetMs (2500), hunter.maxN (truncate),
         hunter.stallDumpMs (600, non-fatal snapshot of a slow iteration), hunter.wdMs (watchdog; sweep: max(10 s, 10x baseline max iteration)).

Workload: per seed a layered DAG, N log-uniform in 10..2000, 3..10 levels, shared children (fan-in) with seed-chosen probability,
node kinds SYNC (runAndWait each kid), FAN (continueWith all, count down), SEQ (continueWith one at a time, default re-enqueue),
MIXED (some sync, some continueWith), ASYNC (exclusive kids launched from a non-graph thread via evaluateNodeAsync or
enqueueAndTrackCompletion), LEAF (every k-th throws), PROMISE (completed after 0..500 us from a non-graph thread with the
scheduler as EvaluationQueue, as NodePromise does); 0..3 nested child cancellation scopes cancelled 0..6 ms into each iteration;
1..6 persistent client threads runAndWait-ing the root or level-1/2 nodes of one shared graph instance. Oracles after every
iteration: every enqueued node done, value == weight + sum(successful kids) (local) and == sequential value when no cancelled
scope is involved, exceptions only from failing leaves or cancelled scopes, no double complete()/run(); H5 after quiescence under
_lock: workers/waiters/externallyBlocked 0, spinningThreadID 0, no runnable task on _qqueue/_queue/idle queues, no tracked nodes
or callbacks left, idle <= 2*ideal, no idle Context with a dead thread, every live og.worker parked and accounted for;
schedulerInBadState() reported. Hang: watchdog dump (stacks, OGScheduler.toString(), waiter snapshot, forward chains, runnable
global work) and classification. Oracle hook in fullWait's deadlock-timer branch counts what the 1 s timer recovers.

Coverage of the final sweep (632 child runs, non-hung ones): 94,054 graph iterations, 20.9 M node enqueues, 2.9 M SEQ re-runs,
1.17 M promise completions from non-graph threads, 605 k external evaluateNodeAsync/enqueueAndTrackCompletion launches,
412 k failing leaves, 44 k cancelled scopes / 50 k cancelled nodes. Seeds with ASYNC nodes: 26/50 (26 with externally launched kids), with PROMISE: 25/50, with cancellation scopes: 25/50.

================================================================ Summary of findings

No lost wakeup found. No H5 accounting violation, no GraphInInvalidState under asserts, no double completion, no wrong value,
no F1b/F2b InfoDumper kill-path event in any of the 632 sweep runs or 900+ reruns. Every failure is a hang or a 1 s stall, of two
new kinds (N2, N3) plus one harness-only observation (N1):

N3 (new, H) Stale causality stamp lets a sync frame run a task that sync-waits on a node suspended lower on the same stack.
   Seeds 14 and 48 (9/12 and 12/14 configs in the final sweep, 5/5 on rerun for almost every failing config), and a deterministic 7-node reproducer (inv).
   Causality ids are sync-stack *depths*, not frame identities. A node stamped (T, d) by a frame at depth d that has since been
   popped is accepted by canTake for any later, unrelated frame of T at depth <= d. The re-enqueue that delivers it comes
   from completing its child on a non-graph thread with the scheduler as EvaluationQueue (NodePromise.complete does exactly this):
   OGScheduler.enqueue hands it straight to the parked waiter (wakeupThread), so the thread runs it inside the unrelated frame;
   its continuation then runAndWaits a shared node S that is itself suspended in a sync frame further down that stack. The
   thread parks on S, S's frame can never return, P2's completion does not wake it (awaitedTask is S, not P2). No cycle in the
   graph, so cycle detection finds nothing; nothing runnable, so the deadlock timer finds nothing. Permanent.
   Dump (seed 14, i1-a0-d0-lt1):
     waiter hunter-client-0 tid=2 parked=true awaitedCausality=3 awaited=HSEQ#14
     chain hunter-client-0: -> HSEQ#14(SRi: 3/1) -> HASYNC#20(SRi: 2/3) -> HMIXED#38(SRi: 2/3)
     SUSPENDED-WITH-KIDS-DONE HMIXED#38 pending=1 [HSYNC#45 done reg=1 notified=1] [HASYNC#46 sync done] [HFAN#48 sync done]
     (HMIXED#38's run() is a frame lower on hunter-client-0's own stack, inside runAndWait(HASYNC#46), done)
   Reproducer schedule (Hunter.inv(), single client T):
     T: runAndWait(R); R: runAndWait(A); A: runAndWait(Q); Q enqueues Z; Z runs in that frame (stamp T/2), registers on promise P1 (100 ms)
     A, Q frames pop; R: runAndWait(S); S: runAndWait(P2 = promise 300 ms) -> T parks at causality 2
     +100 ms P1.complete(scheduler) -> Z.onChildCompleted -> scheduler.enqueue(Z): canTake(Z=2/2, T, 2) -> handed to T
     T runs Z#2: runAndWait(S) -> parks on S, which is suspended under it. INV-HANG Z.done=false S.done=false P1.done=true P2.done=true
   Not on the known list (R1 is id truncation, R6 is frame reuse; here ids are exact and frames are fresh). Fix direction: stamp a
   per-frame identity (monotonic frame id, or depth plus a per-thread epoch bumped on every pop) and compare that in canTake; or have
   enqueue() not hand a task to a waiter whose sync stack contains a node on the task's forward chain.

N2 (new trigger of the mechanism noted at the end of the Tier 1 section, H) limitThreads starvation of global work that no
   waiter may take. Seeds 23, 28, 41, 42, 44, 48 (evaluateNodeAsync-launched work, stamp -1/1 from poisonCausality) and 6, 21, 34,
   39 (SEQ re-enqueues from non-graph threads carrying another thread's stamp, e.g. 3/0, 5/0). Hangs 5/5 on rerun for most configs,
   deterministic 2-node reproducer at ideal=1 (inv n2:...).
   Work lands on the global quick queue with a stamp that canTake rejects for every waiter (poisoned for causality > 0, or another
   thread's id). Only a causality-0 thread could run it, but (a) addWorkerIfNeeded counts waiters against the ideal under
   limitThreads, so with waiters >= ideal no helper is injected (seeds 23/28/41/42/44, reproducer at ideal=1); and (b) the paths
   where a slot frees up do not re-check: nextStepForWorkingThread moving a thread to _waiters calls addWorkerIfNeeded only when
   !limitThreads, exitGraph only when _workers.count > 0 (seed 34: one waiter, ideal 2, two idle workers parked next to a runnable
   SEQ for >= 500 ms). If the task is on a waiter's forward chain the 1 s deadlock timer runs it (PASS-SLOW, timerCS > 0: seeds 6,
   21, 34, 41 at ideal 4); if not, the hang is permanent (seed 39: SEQ#1055 stamped 3/0 by a client that had left, two waiters at
   causality 1, ideal 2, idle worker parked). evaluateNodeAsync's maySAS poisoning is precisely what makes its work untakeable here:
   with -Dhunter.asyncMode=eatc (unpoisoned enqueueAndTrackCompletion) seeds 21/23/28/41/42/44 pass, with ena they all hang.
   limitThreads=false (lt0) configs never hang (0/32 in the sweep): that mode drains the waiter's queue and adds a worker on every
   move to _waiters. Dump (seed 42, i1-a0-d0-lt1):
     HANG-SNAPSHOT workers=0 waiters=4 idle=0 spin=0 qqRunnable=8/8
     qqueue-runnable HLEAF#985 state=N: causality=-1/1 enqueued=true runs=0     (x14)
     waiter hunter-client-0 tid=2 parked=true awaitedCausality=1 awaited=HASYNC#613; chain -> HASYNC#613 -> HLEAF#906(N: 2/1)
   Dump (seed 34 non-fatal stall, i2-a0-d0-lt1, no external threads at all in this seed):
     HANG-SNAPSHOT workers=0 waiters=1 idle=2 spin=0 qqRunnable=1/1
     waiter hunter-client-0 tid=2 parked=true awaitedCausality=1 awaited=HSEQ#3; qqueue-runnable HSEQ#3 state=Si: causality=5/0
   Classification: same family as R1's symptom (runnable work on the global queue, all threads waiting, idle workers parked) and the
   review's Tier 1 remark about limitThreads; but canTake is not lying here (ids are small), so it is a separate hazard. The (b)
   path in seed 34 is inferred from the dump and the code; the scheduled reproducer for it (busy second client leaving the graph)
   did not hang because the spare spinner kept spinning and stole the task, so (b) has organic seeds but no deterministic schedule.
   Fix direction: on every transition that leaves no causality-0 thread (move to waiters, exitGraph, spinner giving up) re-run
   addWorkerIfNeeded if the global queues hold work, and let it exceed ideal when every active thread is a waiter at causality > 0
   (or have fullWait's timer branch check "runnable global work and no worker").

N1 (harness artifact, documents a latent fragility, L) A probes.Delay park placed between fullWait's `if (!ctx.parked)` check and
   its LockSupport.parkNanos consumes the permit left by the waker's unpark, so the real park sleeps the full 1 s: the first
   trial sweep had 1 s stalls with delays on, even at N=2 (seed 1, -Dprobes.delay.points=s.park alone
   reproduces it; all other points without s.park did not). Fixed in the hunter (s.park is a busy spin). Production relevance: with
   schedulerRecoveryInWait (default true) fullWait parks with parkNanos(1 s) without re-checking `parked`, so anything on that
   loop iteration that parks through j.u.c (the timer branch's runOnStalledCallbacks -> user listeners, tryPrintStall's logging)
   turns a wakeup into a 1 s wait. Narrow window, timer-bounded.

Not reproduced / benign
 - Lost wakeup (H3 target): the timer-branch oracle saw the awaited node done at the deadlock tick once in the first sweep and 3 times in
   30 s of dedicated reruns (seed 21, i1-a0-d1-lt1), but in the 3 checked cases ctx.parked was cleared within 20 ms: the wakeup was in flight while the timer fired, not lost (counted as
   timerLWInFlight; timerLW = 0 in the final sweep).
 - seed 20 i4-a1-d1-lt1, first trial sweep only: one NOT_DONE (HASYNC#9, state SRiq, cancelled scopes involved); 0/5 on rerun and
   not seen again in two further full sweeps. No dump (it was an end-of-iteration oracle hit). Open.
 - H5: after every iteration of every passing run the accounting was clean; schedulerInBadState() was false everywhere; live
   og.worker threads always == idle pool size after settling (max 8 at ideal 4).
 - asserts on: no GraphInInvalidState in any configuration, including the hangs.
 - Known list: none of the failures matches R1-R7 or F1-F8 directly; there was no InfoDumper/ExceptionInInitializerError
   (F1b/F2b) in any log, so no hang here is a failed-kill artifact.
 - Minimisation by N (truncating the BFS-ordered DAG) only shrinks seed 48 (596 -> 149, N2); the others need their deep levels,
   so the hand-built reproducers stand in for minimal cases.

Per-finding reproduction
  N3: probes/run-hunter.sh inv i1-a0-d0-lt1            (5/5 hang; ideal 4: 3/5, a spinning worker sometimes steals Z first)
      probes/run-hunter.sh one 14 i1-a0-d0-lt1 0 5     (5/5)   probes/run-hunter.sh one 48 i2-a0-d0-lt1 0 5 (5/5)
      HUNTER_OPTS=-Dhunter.asyncMode=eatc probes/run-hunter.sh one 6 i1-a0-d0-lt1 0 2   (also N3)
  N2: probes/run-hunter.sh inv n2:i1-a0-d0-lt1         (5/5 hang; passes at ideal >= 2 and with lt0)
      probes/run-hunter.sh one 42 i1-a0-d0-lt1 0 5     (5/5)   probes/run-hunter.sh one 39 i2-a0-d0-lt1 0 5 (5/5, no evaluateNodeAsync)
      probes/run-hunter.sh one 34 i2-a0-d0-lt1 0 15    (stall recovered by the timer in ~2/5 JVMs, STALL-DUMP in the log)
  N1: HUNTER_OPTS="-Dhunter.delayPoints=s.park" with a build where s.park calls probes.Delay (as before the fix)
